     - Cause: `EINSUM_MAX_TOTAL_KV=65536` routes bs=64/kv=1k (total_kv=65536) to compiled einsum, which is slower than aiter for this shape.
     - Fix: Lowered `EINSUM_MAX_TOTAL_KV` from 65536 to 32768 so bs=64/kv=1k routes to aiter.

5. Problem Solving:
   
   **SOLVED — Direct ASM call with forced split-K**: Discovered that `gemm_a4w4_asm` with `log2_k_split=2` gives 39% speedup on K=7168 shapes. The auto-tuned config wrongly returns `splitK=0`. By hardcoding kernel names for untuned shapes and forcing split-K for large K, achieved 4.3% GEMM geomean improvement (22.2→21.3µs).
   
   **SOLVED — torch.compile einsum with refined threshold**: torch.compile on the einsum path gives 34% speedup on bs=4/kv=1k. Lowering EINSUM_MAX_TOTAL_KV from 65536 to 32768 avoids regression on bs=64/kv=1k. Net result: 6.1-9.3% MLA geomean improvement (69.2→62.8-65.0µs).
   
   **CONFIRMED — split-K=0 in config is suboptimal**: The `get_GEMM_config` auto-tuned configs all have `splitK=0`, but forcing `log2_k_split=2` for K≥2048 shapes is consistently better. This is a tuning gap in the aiter config database.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user's sole explicit request was: "Continue the conversation from where it left off without asking the user any further questions. Resume directly — do not acknowledge the summary, do not recap what was happening, do not preface with 'I'll continue' or similar. Pick up the last task as if the break never happened."
   
   The underlying task is Phase 16 of the Luma AMD Speedrun competition — optimizing GPU kernels (GEMM, MLA, MoE) for AMD MI355X (gfx950). The session continued from a compacted context where two parallel leaderboard submissions were in progress.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **e8m0_shuffle permutation**: `view(sm//32, 2, 16, sn//8, 2, 4).permute(0, 3, 5, 2, 4, 1).contiguous().view(sm, sn)` — pads M to multiple of 256, K//32 to multiple of 8, then permutes
   - **Fused HIP quant + inline shuffle**: Eliminates the separate e8m0_shuffle call (12.9µs) by computing shuffled output index directly in the GPU kernel
   - **shuffle_index() derivation**: Decomposes `(row, col)` into 6D indices `(d0, d1, d2, d3, d4, d5)` then maps to output flat index using permute strides
   - **Forced split-K for large K**: `log2_k_split=2` for K≥2048, `=1` for K≥1024 (auto-tuned config wrongly returns splitK=0)
   - **gemm_a4w4_asm direct call**: Uses hardcoded kernel names for untuned shapes (`_32x128` for M≤64, `_192x128` for M>64)
   - **torch.compile on einsum**: `mode="max-autotune-no-cudagraphs"` triggers Triton BMM autotuning on the runner
   - **Three-regime MLA routing**: compiled einsum (bs≤4 OR total_kv≤32768), aiter a16w8 (total_kv≤262144), aiter a8w8 (total_kv>262144)
   - **Quant pipeline breakdown** (M=8, K=7168): HIP quant 3.5µs, e8m0_shuffle 12.9µs, GEMM ASM 10.1µs — quant overhead is 78% of total

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission_fused_shuffle.py`** (CREATED, tested ✅ 4/4, benchmarked 16.0µs, leaderboard ✅ 16.9µs ranked)
     - **THE KEY BREAKTHROUGH**: Fuses e8m0_shuffle into HIP quant kernel, eliminating 12.9µs overhead
     - 23.8% improvement over previous best (22.2µs → 16.9µs ranked geomean)
     - Critical HIP device function for computing shuffled output index:
     ```c
     __device__ __forceinline__ int shuffle_index(int row, int col, int sm, int sn) {
         int d0 = row >> 5;           // row / 32
         int r32 = row & 31;          // row % 32
         int d1 = r32 >> 4;           // (row%32) / 16
         int d2 = r32 & 15;           // row % 16
         int d3 = col >> 3;           // col / 8
         int c8 = col & 7;            // col % 8
         int d4 = c8 >> 2;            // (col%8) / 4
         int d5 = c8 & 3;             // col % 4
         int sn8 = sn >> 3;
         int stride_d0 = sn8 * 256;
         int flat = d0 * stride_d0 + d3 * 256 + d5 * 64 + d2 * 4 + d4 * 2 + d1;
         return flat;
     }
     ```
     - The kernel writes scales directly in shuffled layout (lane 0 only):
     ```c
     if (lane == 0) {
         int shuf_idx = shuffle_index(row, grp, sm, sn);
         A_scale_shuffled[shuf_idx] = scale_byte;
     }
     ```
     - Python-side allocates padded output matching e8m0_shuffle dimensions:
     ```python
     def _hip_quantize_fused(A, M, K):
         lib = _ensure_hip()
         if lib is None: return None, None
         A_cont = A.contiguous()
         A_q = torch.empty(M, K // 2, dtype=torch.uint8, device="cuda")
         num_groups = K // 32
         sm = ((M + 255) // 256) * 256
         sn = ((num_groups + 7) // 8) * 8
         A_scale_shuffled = torch.zeros(sm * sn, dtype=torch.uint8, device="cuda")
         err = lib.launch_mxfp4_quant_fused(...)
         return A_q, A_scale_shuffled.view(sm, sn)
     ```

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`** (UPDATED to forced split-K version, needs to be updated again to fused shuffle)
     - Was updated to use `gemm_a4w4_asm` directly with hardcoded kernel names and forced split-K
     - Ranked 22.2µs — now superseded by fused shuffle at 16.9µs
     - Needs to be overwritten with submission_fused_shuffle.py content

   - **`kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py`** (UPDATED to compiled einsum version, tested ✅ 4/4)
     - Uses `torch.compile(_einsum_core, mode="max-autotune-no-cudagraphs")` for small shapes
     - Routing: `bs <= EINSUM_MAX_BS or total_kv <= EINSUM_MAX_TOTAL_KV` (OR, not AND)
     - `EINSUM_MAX_TOTAL_KV = 32768` (lowered from 65536)
     - Leaderboard: 65.0µs ranked geomean (previous session result)

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission_probe_quant_timing.py`** (CREATED, tested ✅ — timing probe)
     - Discovered critical breakdown: HIP quant 3.5µs + e8m0_shuffle 12.9µs + GEMM ASM 10.1µs
     - Triton quant (dynamic_mxfp4_quant) is 22.0µs — 6.3x slower than HIP
     - gemm_a4w4 high-level is 20.4µs vs ASM 10.1µs

5. Problem Solving:

   **SOLVED — e8m0_shuffle overhead elimination (12.9µs → ~0µs)**:
   The e8m0_shuffle was consuming 12.9µs per call — nearly half the non-GEMM pipeline time. By reverse-engineering the shuffle permutation from the runner source code and implementing it as a `shuffle_index()` device function in the HIP quant kernel, the scale bytes are written directly to their shuffled positions. This eliminated a separate kernel launch + memory copy. Result: 23.8% improvement (22.2→16.9µs ranked geomean).
   
   **CONFIRMED — Quant pipeline is the primary bottleneck**:
   The timing probe showed that quantization overhead (Triton quant 22µs or HIP quant 3.5µs + shuffle 12.9µs) dominates the pipeline, with the GEMM ASM kernel itself taking only 10.1µs. The fused approach reduces total non-GEMM overhead from ~16.4µs (HIP quant + shuffle) to ~3.5µs (fused quant+shuffle).
   
   **CONFIRMED — Production files updated**:
   Both MLA (compiled einsum, 65µs) and GEMM (forced split-K, 22.2µs) production submission.py files were updated. The GEMM production file needs another update to the fused shuffle version (16.9µs).

# /learn - Online Learning System

**Extract reusable knowledge from this session into skills.** Evaluates what was learned, checks for existing skills, and creates new ones when valuable patterns are discovered.

---

## TABLE OF CONTENTS

| Phase       | Description                                            | Steps   |
| ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------ | ------- |
| **Phase 0** | Reference: triggers, quality criteria, skill structure | 0.1–0.4 |
| **Phase 1** | Evaluate: assess if knowledge is worth extracting      | 1.1     |
| **Phase 2** | Check Existing: search for related skills              | 2.1     |
| **Phase 3** | Create Skill: write the skill file                     | 3.1–3.2 |
| **Phase 4** | Quality Gates: final checklist before saving           | 4.1     |

**Quick Step Reference:**

- 0.1 Triggers, 0.2 Quality criteria, 0.3 What NOT to extract, 0.4 Skill structure
- 1.1 Self-evaluation questions
- 2.1 Search existing skills
- 3.1 Choose location, 3.2 Write SKILL.md
- 4.1 Final checklist

---

## PHASE 0: REFERENCE

### Step 0.1: Triggers

Invoke `/learn` after ANY task involving:

| Trigger                      | Example                                                          |
| ---------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Non-obvious debugging**    | Spent 10+ minutes investigating; solution wasn't in docs         |
| **Misleading errors**        | Error message pointed wrong direction; found real cause          |
| **Workarounds**              | Found limitation and creative solution                           |
| **Tool integration**         | Figured out how to use tool/API in undocumented way              |
| **Trial-and-error**          | Tried multiple approaches before finding what worked             |
| **Repeatable workflow**      | Multi-step task that will recur; worth standardizing             |
| **External service queries** | Fetched data from Jira, GitHub, Confluence, or other APIs        |
| **User-facing automation**   | Built something user will ask for again (reports, status checks) |

### Step 0.2: Quality Criteria

Skills must be:

- **Reusable**: Will help with future tasks (not just this instance)
- **Non-trivial**: Required discovery, OR is a valuable workflow pattern
- **Verified**: Solution actually worked, not theoretical

### Step 0.3: What NOT to Extract

- Single-step tasks with no workflow value
- One-off fixes unlikely to recur
- Knowledge easily found in official docs

### Step 0.4: Skill Structure

**Location:** `.claude/skills/[skill-name]/SKILL.md`

**Template:**

```markdown
---
name: descriptive-kebab-case-name
description: |
  [CRITICAL: This determines when the skill triggers. Include:]
  - What the skill does
  - Specific trigger conditions (exact error messages, symptoms)
  - When to use it (contexts, scenarios)
author: Claude Code
version: 1.0.0
---

# Skill Name

## Problem

[Clear description of the problem]

## Context / Trigger Conditions

[When to use - exact error messages, symptoms, scenarios]

## Solution

[Step-by-step solution]

## Verification

[How to verify it worked]

## Example

[Concrete example]

## References

[Links to documentation]
```

<details>
<summary>Writing Effective Descriptions</summary>

The description field is CRITICAL for skill discovery:

**Good:**

```yaml
description: |
  Fix for "ENOENT: no such file or directory" errors in npm monorepos.
  Use when: (1) npm run fails with ENOENT, (2) paths work in root but
  not in packages, (3) symlinked dependencies cause failures.
```

**Bad:**

```yaml
description: Helps with npm problems in monorepos.
```

</details>

**Guidelines:**

- **Concise** - Claude is smart; only add what it doesn't know
- **Under 500 lines** - Move large docs to `references/`
- **Examples over explanations** - Show, don't tell

---

## PHASE 1: EVALUATE

### Step 1.1: Self-Evaluation Questions

Ask yourself:

1. "What did I just learn that wasn't obvious before starting?"
2. "Would future-me benefit from having this documented?"
3. "Was the solution non-obvious from documentation alone?"
4. "Is this a multi-step workflow I'd repeat on similar tasks?"
5. "Did I query an external service the user will ask about again?"

**If NO to all → Skip extraction, nothing to learn.**

**Note:** External service queries (Jira tickets, GitHub PRs, Confluence pages) are almost always worth extracting - users frequently repeat these requests.

### Step 3.1: Choose Location

   1. Copy `submission_fused_shuffle.py` to `kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py` as production (pending from previous session)
   2. Document the e8m0_shuffle fusion technique in the skills system
   3. Explore further GEMM optimizations — focusing on allocation overhead and split-K tuning

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **e8m0_shuffle fusion**: HIP device function `shuffle_index()` computes the permuted output index inline during quantization, eliminating a separate 12.9µs kernel launch
   - **Static buffer pre-allocation**: Pre-allocate A_q, scale, and output buffers per (M, K) / (M, N) shape key; initialize scale buffer once with `torch.zeros` — reuse without re-zeroing, saving 3-5µs per call
   - **No-zeroing safety**: The HIP quant kernel visits every `(row, grp)` in `[0..M) × [0..K//32)`, so the active scale region is fully overwritten each call; padding positions stay 0 from initial allocation
   - **log2_k_split sweep**: All log2_k_split values (None, 0, 1, 2, 3, 4) produce essentially the same GEMM time (6.8–7.6µs) — split-K has negligible effect for these shapes
   - **torch.zeros cost**: Fresh `torch.zeros(57344 bytes)` costs 4.98µs; pre-alloc `zero_()` costs 3.18µs; pre-alloc no zeroing costs 0µs (savings: 3-5µs per call)
   - **gemm_a4w4_asm**: Direct ASM GEMM call with `bpreshuffle=True`, hardcoded kernel names for untuned shapes
   - **Split-K tuning update**: K≥4096 → log2_ks=3, K≥2048 → log2_ks=2, K≥1024 → log2_ks=1 (updated from previous K≥2048 → 2)
   - **Benchmark methodology**: Single-call with L2 cache clear between iterations — no amortization of Python overhead

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`** (UPDATED TWICE)
     - First update: copied from `submission_fused_shuffle.py` (fused e8m0 shuffle HIP kernel)
     - Second update (end of session): copied from `submission_static_bufs.py` (adds static buffer pre-allocation)
     - Current production: 13.8µs ranked geomean
     - Key `_get_buffers` function:
     ```python
     _A_q_buf: dict = {}
     _scale_buf: dict = {}
     _out_buf: dict = {}

     def _get_buffers(M, K, N):
         mk_key = (M, K)
         if mk_key not in _A_q_buf:
             _A_q_buf[mk_key] = torch.empty(M, K // 2, dtype=torch.uint8, device="cuda")
         if mk_key not in _scale_buf:
             sm = ((M + 255) // 256) * 256
             sn = ((K // 32 + 7) // 8) * 8
             _scale_buf[mk_key] = torch.zeros(sm * sn, dtype=torch.uint8, device="cuda")
         mn_key = (M, N)
         if mn_key not in _out_buf:
             _out_buf[mn_key] = torch.empty(M, N, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda")
         return _A_q_buf[mk_key], _scale_buf[mk_key], _out_buf[mn_key]
     ```
     - Key `custom_kernel` flow:
     ```python
     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
         M, K = A.shape
         N = B.shape[0]
         lib = _ensure_hip()
         if lib is not None:
             num_groups_k = K // 32
             sm = ((M + 255) // 256) * 256
             sn = ((num_groups_k + 7) // 8) * 8
             A_q, scale_flat, out = _get_buffers(M, K, N)
             A_cont = A.contiguous()
             err = lib.launch_mxfp4_quant_fused(
                 ctypes.c_void_p(A_cont.data_ptr()),
                 ctypes.c_void_p(A_q.data_ptr()),
                 ctypes.c_void_p(scale_flat.data_ptr()),
                 ctypes.c_int(M), ctypes.c_int(K),
                 ctypes.c_int(sm), ctypes.c_int(sn),
             )
             if err == 0:
                 A_q_fp4x2 = A_q.view(dtypes.fp4x2)
                 A_scale_sh = scale_flat.view(sm, sn).view(dtypes.fp8_e8m0)
                 kernel_name, log2_ks = _get_config(M, N, K)
                 return aiter.gemm_a4w4_asm(
                     A_q_fp4x2, B_shuffle, A_scale_sh, B_scale_sh,
                     out, kernel_name, bpreshuffle=True, log2_k_split=log2_ks,
                 )
     ```
     - Updated split-K logic:
     ```python
     if K >= 4096:
         log2_ks = 3
     elif K >= 2048:
         log2_ks = 2
     elif K >= 1024:
         log2_ks = 1
     else:
         log2_ks = None
     ```

Summary:

   The underlying objective is Phase 16 of the Luma AMD Speedrun competition: optimize GPU kernels for AMD MI355X (gfx950), specifically the MXFP4 GEMM (`amd-mxfp4-mm` leaderboard). The session started with `submission.py` at 13.8µs ranked geomean and continued investigating the M=16, K=7168 shape outlier at 22.6µs.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **MXFP4 GEMM pipeline**: bf16 A → HIP fused quant+shuffle → `gemm_a4w4_asm` → bf16 output
   - **`gemm_a4w4` vs `gemm_a4w4_asm`**: `gemm_a4w4` auto-selects kernel; `gemm_a4w4_asm` requires explicit kernel name + `log2_k_split`. Both use the same underlying ASM kernels (`_KERNEL_32x128`, `_KERNEL_192x128`) — both are flying blind since ALL competition shapes print "not found tuned config in CKGEMM or asmGEMM"
   - **Split-K (log2_k_split)**: Splits K dimension into 2^n chunks for parallel reduction. Increases block count to fill CUs. BUT the reduce kernel overhead dominates for large M shapes where N-tiles already provide sufficient CU utilization
   - **CU utilization analysis**: For M=4 with `_KERNEL_32x128`: only 23 N-tiles × no K-split = 23 blocks on 256 CUs (9% utilization). For M=64: 2×57=114 tiles, adding log2_ks=2 gives 456 blocks but adds reduce overhead
   - **M-aware split-K heuristic**: Large M (≥64): N-tiles alone fill CUs; split-K reduce overhead dominates → use None. Small M (≤32): split-K needed to fill CUs
   - **Static buffer pre-allocation**: Per (M,K) key for A_q and scale; per (M,N) for output. Scale initialized once with `torch.zeros`, reused without re-zeroing
   - **Ranked vs benchmark geomean**: Ranked benchmark uses more runs with heavier measurement overhead than the inline benchmark; results can differ by ~1µs per shape

               if err == 0:
                   A_q_fp4x2 = A_q.view(dtypes.fp4x2)

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`** (MODIFIED AND LEADERBOARD SUBMITTED)
     - Current production file based on `submission_static_bufs.py` with additional optimizations
     - The file had already been modified (from between sessions): `_get_config` now reads `splitK` from `get_GEMM_config` dict and has variable named `split_k` instead of `log2_ks`
     - Key change made in this session: added M≥64 → `split_k=None` guard
     - Modified `_get_config` function:
       ```python
       def _get_config(M, N, K):
           key = (M, N, K)
           if key not in _config_cache:
               kernel_name = None
               split_k = None
               try:
                   cfg = get_GEMM_config(M, N, K)
                   if isinstance(cfg, dict):
                       kn = cfg.get('kernelName')
                       if kn is not None:
                           kernel_name = str(kn)
                       sk = cfg.get('splitK')
                       if sk is not None and int(sk) > 0:
                           split_k = int(sk)
                   elif cfg is not None:
                       kernel_name = str(cfg)
               except Exception:
                   pass

               if kernel_name is None:
                   if M <= 64:
                       kernel_name = _KERNEL_32x128
                   else:
                       kernel_name = _KERNEL_192x128

               # Prefer config-driven splitK; fall back to heuristic.
               # Large M (≥64): N-tiles alone fill CUs; split-K reduce overhead dominates → no split.
               # Small M (≤32): few N-tiles → split-K fills CUs with more work items.
               if split_k is None:
                   if M >= 64:
                       split_k = None
                   elif K >= 4096:
                       split_k = 3
                   elif K >= 2048:
                       split_k = 2
                   elif K >= 1024:
                       split_k = 1

               _config_cache[key] = (kernel_name, split_k)
           return _config_cache[key]
       ```
     - Leaderboard results (ranked benchmark):
       - M=4, K=512: 11.4µs
       - M=16, K=7168: 21.5µs
       - M=32, K=512, N=4096: 11.8µs
       - M=32, K=512, N=2880: 11.7µs
       - M=64, K=2048: 14.6µs
       - M=256, K=1536: 13.3µs

   **INVESTIGATED — We were slower than aiter baseline on ALL shapes**:

   **SOLVED — Large M split-K overhead (M≥64)**:
   For M=64, K=2048 with `_KERNEL_32x128`: 2×57=114 N-tiles, enough to fill CUs without split-K. Adding log2_ks=2 (4-way) creates 456 blocks but requires a reduce step whose overhead dominates. Setting log2_ks=None saves 0.6-0.8µs per large-M shape.

# aiter Kernel Parameter Semantics (AMD MI355X)

## Problem

aiter's `fused_moe` and `mla_decode_fwd` have parameters that sound like performance flags
but actually change computation or have counter-intuitive effects on MI355X.

## fused_moe: doweight_stage1

### What it does

`doweight_stage1=True` moves the `topk_weights` multiplication from **stage 2** (after SiLU
activation) to **stage 1** (before SiLU activation).

- `doweight_stage1=False` (default): `output = SiLU(gate(x)) * up(x)`, then multiply weights in stage 2
- `doweight_stage1=True`: multiply weights in stage 1 BEFORE activation

### Why it causes correctness failure

SiLU is a **nonlinear** activation. Multiplying weights before vs after a nonlinear activation
produces mathematically different results:

```
w * SiLU(gate(x)) * up(x)   ≠   SiLU(w * gate(x)) * (w * up(x))
```

This is not a subtle numerical difference — it produces ~80% element mismatches with 10-30%
per-element error (well above the 5e-2 tolerance for MoE).

**Rule:** `doweight_stage1=True` only produces correct results for linear activations (ReLU-like),
not for SiLU/GELU. For MXFP4 MoE with SiLU, always use `doweight_stage1=False`.

## fused_moe: OPUS Sorting (AITER_USE_OPUS_MOE_SORTING)

Setting `os.environ["AITER_USE_OPUS_MOE_SORTING"] = "1"` before calling `fused_moe` switches
to the OPUS sorting backend (`moe_sorting_opus_fwd` instead of `moe_sorting_fwd`).

**Correctness:** OPUS sorting only changes token dispatch order — the computation is identical.
Setting this env var passes correctness tests (verified, max error 0.015625).

**Performance on MXFP4 variant:** NO EFFECT. The env var is not wired into the fp4x2 code path
in `fused_moe.py`. JIT still builds `module_moe_sorting` (not `module_moe_sorting_opus`), and
benchmark times are identical to reference. This was verified by running the full benchmark suite.

## fused_moe: block_size_M

Override the auto-selected tile size for the token dimension:

```python
fused_moe(..., block_size_M=64)  # or 32, 128
```

Default is `-1` (auto-selects from tuned CSV configs). For MXFP4 with different model configs:
- bs=8 (small): auto-selects `block_m=32`
- bs=128 (medium): auto-selects `block_m=64`

**DANGER: Forcing `block_size_M=64` causes GPU memory access faults on benchmark configs.**
When `estimated_m_per_expert` is low (e.g., bs=16 with 256 experts = ~1 token/expert), a tile
size of 64 exceeds the actual data bounds and causes a hardware-level `Memory access fault by
GPU node-2`. The auto-tuner correctly selects smaller tiles for these shapes. **Never override
`block_size_M` without verifying it's safe for ALL shapes in the benchmark suite.**

## mla_decode_fwd: fast_mode is SLOWER on MI355X

### What it does

`fast_mode=True` in `get_mla_metadata_info_v1` and `get_mla_metadata_v1` changes the work
distribution strategy: `(bs + cu_num) * tiles` work items vs `tile_cnt * cu_num`.

### Test results (MI355X, DeepSeek R1 MLA, fp8)

| Config | fast_mode=False (reference) | fast_mode=True, adaptive splits | fast_mode=True, fixed splits=32 |
|--------|----------------------------|--------------------------------|--------------------------------|
| bs=4, kv=1024 | ~131 us | ~154 us | ~152 us |
| bs=256, kv=8192 | ~462 us | ~536 us | ~542 us |
| Geomean | ~191 us | ~224 us (+17%) | ~232 us (+21%) |

**fast_mode=True is 17-21% SLOWER than fast_mode=False on MI355X for decode workloads.**

The reference implementation already uses well-tuned defaults. Changing `fast_mode` to `True`
and `num_kv_splits` from 32 to adaptive values (4-16 depending on batch size) both made
performance worse, not better.

**Rule:** For MLA decode on MI355X with the DeepSeek R1 forward_absorb architecture, use:
```python
fast_mode=False
num_kv_splits=32
intra_batch_mode=True
```

## fused_moe: AITER_KSPLIT (Environment Variable)

`AITER_KSPLIT=N` switches `fused_moe` between two entirely different kernel implementations:

- **KSPLIT=0 (default / unset):** Uses `module_moe_ck2stages_fp4x2_fp4x2_preshuffle_*` — MXFP4-optimized CK kernels, fast for dense token distributions
- **KSPLIT>0:** Uses `module_moe_cktile2stages` — generic cktile kernels (not MXFP4-specialized), but better for sparse token distributions

**Critical:** AITER_KSPLIT is **completely ignored** for shapes with pre-tuned CSV configs (e.g., 257-expert DeepSeek-V3 configs in `tuned_fmoe.csv`). Only affects shapes that fall back to default kernel selection.

### Benchmark Data (MI355X, dhidden=7168, with AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG=1)

**33-expert shapes (dexp=512/2048):**

| Shape | KSPLIT=0 | KSPLIT=2 | Winner |
|-------|----------|----------|--------|
| bs=16, 33 exp | 94.5 µs | 60.6 µs | cktile (-36%) |
| bs=128, 33 exp | 130 µs | 109 µs | cktile (-16%) |
| bs=512, 33 exp, dexp=512 | 214 µs | 253 µs | CK (+18%) |
| bs=512, 33 exp, dexp=2048 | 352 µs | 703 µs | CK (+100%) |

**257-expert shapes (dexp=256, TP=8 DeepSeek-V3) — BYPASS_TUNE required:**

| Shape | CK (CSV, est_m) | KSPLIT=2 | KSPLIT=4 | Winner |
|-------|---------|----------|----------|--------|
| bs=16, 257E (est_m≈0) | 152.7 µs | 95.1 µs | 91.3 µs | KSPLIT=4 (-40% vs CK) |
| bs=128, 257E (est_m≈4) | 239 µs | 186 µs | 172 µs | KSPLIT=4 (-28% vs CK) |
| bs=512, 257E (est_m≈17) | 336.5 µs | 275 µs | 284 µs | KSPLIT=2 (-18% vs CK) |

**Full 7-shape competition benchmark (geomean target: ~155 µs vs ~145 µs leader):**

| Shape | Reference | Phase 8 (BYPASS+K2) | Phase 10 (expert-aware) |
|-------|-----------|---------------------|------------------------|
| bs=16, 257E | 152.7 µs | 95.1 µs | 91.6 µs (KSPLIT=4) |
| bs=128, 257E | 239 µs | 186 µs | 171 µs (KSPLIT=4) |
| bs=512, 257E | 336.5 µs | 275 µs | 284 µs (KSPLIT=2) |
| bs=16, 33E | 106.2 µs | 59.8 µs | 59.8 µs (KSPLIT=2) |
| bs=128, 33E | 141.1 µs | 109 µs | 109 µs (KSPLIT=2) |
| bs=512, 33E dexp=512 | 225 µs | 209 µs | 214 µs (KSPLIT=0) |
| bs=512, 33E dexp=2048 | 380.4 µs | 343 µs | 355 µs (KSPLIT=0) |
| **Geomean** | ~206 µs | ~156 µs | ~155 µs |

**Rule:** `estimated_m_per_expert = topk_ids.numel() // num_experts`
- `estimated_m >= 50` → KSPLIT unset (CK path, dense shapes)
- `num_experts >= 200 AND estimated_m < 10` → KSPLIT=4 (large expert count, tiny per-expert matrices benefit from K-parallelism)
- `estimated_m < 50` (other) → KSPLIT=2 (cktile path, moderate sparse)

**Why KSPLIT=4 helps for 257E but not 33E:** 257E shapes have dexp=256 (small weight matrices per expert). With K=256, KSPLIT=4 splits K into 64-element chunks which fits better in cache. 33E shapes have dexp=512+ (larger matrices where KSPLIT=2 already saturates K-parallelism).

### Expert-Count-Aware Adaptive KSPLIT Pattern

Setting env var per-call works because AITER checks it at dispatch time (not module import):

```python
import os

num_experts = gate_up_weight_shuffled.shape[0]
estimated_m = topk_ids.numel() // num_experts

if estimated_m >= 50:
    # Dense: CK MXFP4-optimized path
    os.environ.pop("AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG", None)
    os.environ.pop("AITER_KSPLIT", None)
elif num_experts >= 200 and estimated_m < 10:
    # Large expert count, very sparse: KSPLIT=4 for K-parallelism
    os.environ["AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG"] = "1"
    os.environ["AITER_KSPLIT"] = "4"
else:
    # Moderately sparse or small expert count: KSPLIT=2
    os.environ["AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG"] = "1"
    os.environ["AITER_KSPLIT"] = "2"

return fused_moe(...)
```

### Stderr Diagnostics: Reading Kernel Path Selection

The aiter log output tells you definitively which path was taken — critical for debugging KSPLIT behavior:

**CSV-tuned shape (KSPLIT ignored):**
```
[aiter] [fused_moe] using 2stage (kernelName1='moe_ck2stages_gemm1_256x32x128x128_...', kernelName2='...') for (...)
```
The parenthetical `(kernelName1=...)` means a specific CSV-tuned kernel was selected. AITER_KSPLIT has no effect here.

**Default shape (KSPLIT applies):**
```
[aiter] run_1stage = False, ksplit = 2 q_type = QuantType.per_1x32 block_m = 32 use_nt = True, estimated_m_per_expert = 4
[aiter] [fused_moe] using 2stage default for (...)
```
The `using 2stage default` message confirms KSPLIT was read and applied. `ksplit = 2` confirms the cktile path was selected.

**Key log fields:**
- `ksplit = 0` → MXFP4-optimized CK path (KSPLIT unset or =0)
- `ksplit = 2` → cktile path (AITER_KSPLIT=2)
- `use_nt = True` → non-temporal memory loads enabled (AITER_USE_NT env var)
- `estimated_m_per_expert = N` → the computed token density value

### JIT Cost of KSPLIT>0

Enabling KSPLIT>0 for the first time triggers **additional JIT builds**:
- `module_moe_cktile2stages`: ~110s
- `module_activation`: ~22s

Total additional JIT time: ~132s. Combined with base MoE JIT (~128s), this pushes total JIT to ~260s, leaving ~7.5 min of compute time in a 12-min Popcorn workflow.

## fused_moe: AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG (Environment Variable)

`AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG=1` forces the **heuristic path** for all shapes, even those with pre-tuned CSV configs:

```python
if cfg is None or int(os.environ.get("AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG", "0")):
    # Heuristic path — AITER_KSPLIT applies here
    ksplit = get_ksplit(token, topk, expert, inter_dim, model_dim)
    ...
```

**Use case:** When CSV-tuned configs exist (e.g., 257-expert DeepSeek TP=8 shapes) but you want to test the cktile (split_k) path for cold-cache benchmarks.

Combined strategy:
```python
if estimated_m < 50:
    os.environ["AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG"] = "1"  # Ignore CSV for sparse shapes
    os.environ["AITER_KSPLIT"] = "2"              # Force cktile path
else:
    os.environ.pop("AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG", None)
    os.environ.pop("AITER_KSPLIT", None)           # CK path for dense
```

**Risk:** CSV configs were pre-tuned (possibly at hot cache). Cold-cache benchmarks may or may not prefer cktile — requires empirical testing.

**AITER_ONLINE_TUNE Warning:** `AITER_ONLINE_TUNE=1` blocks on running `gemm_moe_tune.py` (a full benchmark sweep) before returning for the FIRST call. This adds many minutes to the first call and will TIMEOUT a 12-minute workflow.

## fused_moe: cktile Path Trigger Conditions

The cktile kernels (`cktile_moe_stage1` + `cktile_moe_stage2`) are used instead of CK when ALL of:
1. `dtype in [bf16, fp16]`
2. `q_type == QuantType.per_1x32` (MXFP4)
3. `q_dtype_w in [dtypes.fp4x2]` (MXFP4 weights)
4. **`ksplit > 1`** — set by AITER_KSPLIT or get_ksplit()
5. **`is_shuffled=True`** — requires pre-shuffled weights (shuffled variants in submission)

If any condition is false, the CK path is used. Using `gate_up_weight_shuffled` and `down_weight_shuffled` (shuffled=True) is required for cktile.

## fused_moe: Parameter Quick Reference

| Parameter | Default | Effect | Safe to Change? |
|-----------|---------|--------|----------------|
| `doweight_stage1` | `False` | **Changes computation** — must be `False` for SiLU | NO (SiLU fails) |
| `block_size_M` | `-1` (auto) | Changes CK tile size | **DANGEROUS** — GPU memory fault if too large for shape |
| `moe_sorting_dispatch_policy` | `0` | Token dispatch strategy | Yes but 20-79% SLOWER at policy=1 |
| `AITER_USE_OPUS_MOE_SORTING` | unset | Dispatch backend | No effect on MXFP4 variant |
| `AITER_KSPLIT` | unset (=0) | **Switches kernel implementation** — see above | Yes (adaptive per estimated_m) |
| `AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG` | unset | Ignore CSV pre-tuned configs, use heuristic | Yes (use with KSPLIT=2 for CSV-locked shapes) |
| `AITER_ONLINE_TUNE` | unset | Block on tuning run before first call | **NO** — will timeout 12-min workflow |

## mla_decode_fwd: Parameter Quick Reference

| Parameter | Reference Value | Effect | Change? |
|-----------|----------------|--------|---------|
| `fast_mode` | `False` | Work distribution strategy | NO — slower on MI355X |
| `num_kv_splits` | `32` | Split count for KV attention | Tested 4-16, all slower |
| `intra_batch_mode` | `True` | Intra-batch parallelism | Do not change |
| `page_size` | `1` | KV page size | Do not change |

## aiter CSV Config File Structure

aiter stores pre-tuned kernel configurations in CSV files under `<aiter_path>/configs/`. These are **merged at startup** and determine which shapes are "CSV-tuned" (AITER_KSPLIT ignored) vs "default" (AITER_KSPLIT applies):

| File | Contents | Quant Type |
|------|----------|------------|
| `tuned_fmoe.csv` | Generic GPU configs (cu_num=80), bf16/fp8/int8 | NOT MXFP4 |
| `dsv3_fp4_tuned_fmoe.csv` | DeepSeek-V3 257-expert MXFP4 configs | `per_1x32` (MXFP4) |
| `a8w8_blockscale_tuned_fmoe_qwen3_235b.csv` | Qwen3 configs | int8 block-scale |
| `untuned_fmoe.csv` | Shapes needing tuning (not yet profiled) | various |

**Critical implication:** The 257-expert DeepSeek shapes used by the Luma AMD benchmark are tuned via `dsv3_fp4_tuned_fmoe.csv`. AITER_KSPLIT is completely ignored for these shapes. Only 33-expert or other non-DeepSeek shapes fall through to the "default" path where AITER_KSPLIT applies.

### Introspection Probe Stderr Truncation Warning

When dumping aiter CSV configs in a Popcorn submission (via stderr print), **large CSV files overflow the stderr buffer** and truncate subsequent output. To avoid this when introspecting:

1. **Never dump all CSVs** — filter to only the target file (e.g., `'dsv3_fp4' in f.lower()`)
2. **Separate probes for CSVs vs source code** — run CSV dump probe and source-code probe as separate submissions
3. **Print source code first** — if you must combine, print `inspect.getsource()` before CSV content

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **MXFP4 GEMM pipeline**: bf16 A → HIP fused quant+shuffle kernel → `gemm_a4w4_asm` with pre-shuffled B → bf16 output
   - **Two available ASM kernel names** (exhaustive — no others found via inspect.getsource):
     - `_KERNEL_32x128 = 'REDACTED'`
     - `_KERNEL_192x128 = 'REDACTED'`
   - **`log2_k_split` parameter**: splits K dimension into 2^n chunks for parallel reduction; adds a separate reduce kernel
   - **Static buffer pre-allocation**: Per (M,K) key for A_q and scale; per (M,N) for output — eliminates ~3-5µs allocation overhead per call
   - **CU utilization**: MI355X has 256 CUs. Base blocks = N-tiles × M-tiles (with benchmark shapes often having very low base count, e.g., M=256 has only 48 blocks)
   - **Cold vs warm L2 cache**: Benchmark clears L2 between timing runs; probe sequential measurements have warm L2 — making probe GEMM-only times inconsistent with total benchmark times
   - **Reference pre-compilation**: The eval harness calls `ref_kernel()` before `custom_kernel()`, pre-compiling `gemm_a4w4` and Triton quant modules. Our `submission.py` can rely on these being pre-compiled.
   - **CUDA event timing**: `t.elapsed_time(t1)` returns milliseconds; `* 1000` converts to µs

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **Probe triggered on wrong shape (test mode)**: Probe used `_call_count == 5` globally, but test mode shapes didn't include M=16, K=7168. Fixed by replacing with per-shape set `_probed_shapes: set = set()` and triggering on `shape_key not in _probed_shapes`.
   - **ks=4 regression for M=16**: Changed K≥4096 from `split_k=3` to `split_k=4` based on hot-cache GEMM probe showing 12.24µs vs 12.56µs. Leaderboard showed M=16 WORSE (22.4µs vs 21.5µs). Root cause: cold-L2 reduce overhead for 16-way K-split (combining 16 partial matrices × 16×2112×2B = ~1MB) exceeds the GEMM kernel speedup. Reverted to `split_k=3`.
   - **`Read` tool parameter error**: Used `start_line`/`end_line` params which aren't supported. Used the tool without those params instead.

5. Problem Solving:
   **INVESTIGATED — Why M=16, K=7168 is slower than reference (20.873µs)**:
   - Our best: 21.5µs ranked (HIP quant + `gemm_a4w4_asm` 32x128 ks=3)
   - The reference uses Triton quant + `gemm_a4w4` auto-select
   - GEMM probe confirmed ks=3 is near-optimal for M=16 (12.56µs vs ks=4=12.24µs hot, but cold benchmark shows ks=4 is worse)
   - Gap of ~0.5-1µs appears irreducible without different kernel architecture

   **CONFIRMED — `gemm_a4w4` auto-select timing**: The 157µs seen in probe was JIT compilation. With reference pre-compiling, it's fast. But `gemm_a4w4` lacks static `out` buffer support, adding ~1µs allocation overhead vs `gemm_a4w4_asm`.

   **IDENTIFIED — M=64 and M=256 are significantly behind reference**:
   - M=64: we=14.6µs ranked, reference=12.738µs → 1.9µs gap
   - M=256: we=13.3µs ranked, reference=12.219µs → 1.1µs gap
   - Root cause: our `M≥64 → ks=None` guard leaves very low CU utilization (44% for M=64, 19% for M=256)
   - Probe suggests `192x128, ks=2` for both shapes fills CUs better (224 blocks for M=64, 192 blocks for M=256)
   - However probe measurements are unreliable for absolute comparison (sequential warm L2 measurements exceed total benchmark times — inconsistency explained by harness wall-clock measuring with async GPU launches)

       ```python
       @helion.kernel()
       def mxfp4_gemm(
           A_q: torch.Tensor,      # [M, K_HALF] packed fp4 (uint8), K_HALF = K//2
           A_scale: torch.Tensor,   # [M, K_SCALE] e8m0 scales (uint8), K_SCALE = K_HALF//16
           B_q: torch.Tensor,       # [K_HALF, N] packed fp4 (uint8), B transposed
           B_scale: torch.Tensor,   # [N, K_SCALE] e8m0 scales (uint8)
       ) -> torch.Tensor:
           M, K_HALF = A_q.shape
           _, N = B_q.shape
           C = hl.zeros([M, N], dtype=torch.float32)
           for tile_m, tile_n in hl.tile([M, N]):
               acc = hl.zeros([tile_m, tile_n], dtype=torch.float32)
               for tile_k in hl.tile(K_HALF):
                   a_tile = A_q[tile_m, tile_k]
                   b_tile = B_q[tile_k, tile_n]
                   a_scale_tile = A_scale[tile_m, tile_k // 16]
                   b_scale_tile = B_scale[tile_n, tile_k // 16]
                   acc = hl.dot_scaled(
                       a_tile, a_scale_tile, "e2m1",
                       b_tile, b_scale_tile, "e2m1",
                       acc=acc,
                   )
               C[tile_m, tile_n] = acc.to(torch.bfloat16)
           return C
       ```

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The sole explicit user message was: "Continue the conversation from where it left off without asking the user any further questions. Resume directly — do not acknowledge the summary, do not recap what was happening, do not preface with 'I'll continue' or similar. Pick up the last task as if the break never happened."

   The underlying objective is Phase 16 of the Luma AMD Speedrun competition on the `amd-mxfp4-mm` leaderboard (AMD MI355X, gfx950). Current score: ~13.7µs geomean vs leader 9.7µs. Task #11 (in_progress): explore further GEMM optimization opportunities via Helion-generated Triton kernels.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Helion tile proxy system**: `Tile` is a `torch.Tensor` subclass; `tile_k // 16` calls `__torch_function__`, converts Tile → SymInt via `_tile_to_size`, then does floor division → produces `BLOCK_K_symint // 16` (a derived FloorDiv sympy expression, NOT a plain `sympy.Symbol`)
   - **`SubscriptIndexing.compute_shape`**: Only appends an output dimension for a `torch.SymInt` index if its sympy expression `isinstance(symbol, sympy.Symbol)` AND has `BlockSizeOrigin`. A derived expression like `BLOCK_K // 16` fails this test → dimension eliminated → 1D tensor
   - **Root cause of `mat1_scale must be a 2D tensor, got 1D`**: `A_scale[tile_m, tile_k // 16]` → second index is derived SymInt, not a plain block-size symbol → 1D result
   - **Fix**: Full-slice approach `A_scale[tile_m, :]` uses `isinstance(k, slice)` branch in `compute_shape` → creates a "reduction dimension" via `env.allocate_reduction_dimension(K_SCALE)` → 2D `[BLOCK_M, K_SCALE]` tensor → passes `ndim != 2` check
   - **MXFP4 GEMM pipeline**: bf16 A → HIP fused quant+shuffle → `gemm_a4w4_asm` with pre-shuffled B → bf16 output
   - **`tl.dot_scaled` format**: `tl.dot_scaled(a, a_scale, 'e2m1', b, b_scale, 'e2m1')` — exact call Helion emits
   - **Helion reduction dimensions**: Full-slice `[:]` on a tensor of shape K creates `_RDIM_SIZE_N = K` as a `tl.constexpr` passed to the kernel, and uses `tl.arange(0, _RDIM_SIZE_N)` for the indices
   - **Block sizes in Helion codegen**: `config = helion.Config(block_sizes=[block_m, block_n], ...)` for the full-slice version (no block_k since K is not tiled)

3. Files and Code Sections:

   - **`research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/helion_gemm_gen.py`** (COMPLETELY REWRITTEN)
     - Previous version used `A_scale[tile_m, tile_k // 16]` → caused `mat1_scale must be a 2D tensor, got 1D` error
     - New version uses full-slice approach `A_scale[tile_m, :]` — no inner K loop, loads entire K dimension at once
     - Used K=128 (tiny) for tracing to generate the code template; actual K loop added manually later
     - `Config(block_sizes=[block_m, block_n], ...)` — removed block_k since K is not tiled by Helion
     ```python
     @helion.kernel()
     def mxfp4_gemm(
         A_q: torch.Tensor,      # [M, K_HALF] packed fp4 (uint8), K_HALF = K//2
         A_scale: torch.Tensor,   # [M, K_SCALE] e8m0 scales (uint8), K_SCALE = K_HALF//16
         B_q: torch.Tensor,       # [K_HALF, N] packed fp4 (uint8)
         B_scale: torch.Tensor,   # [N, K_SCALE] e8m0 scales (uint8)
     ) -> torch.Tensor:
         M, K_HALF = A_q.shape
         _, N = B_q.shape
         C = hl.zeros([M, N], dtype=torch.float32)
         for tile_m, tile_n in hl.tile([M, N]):
             # Full-slice K: A_scale[tile_m, :] → [BLOCK_M, K_SCALE] (2D, passes ndim check)
             # tile_k // 16 would produce a derived SymInt (not BlockSizeOrigin) → 1D → fails
             a_tile = A_q[tile_m, :]           # [BLOCK_M, K_HALF]
             a_scale_tile = A_scale[tile_m, :] # [BLOCK_M, K_SCALE] — 2D ✓
             b_tile = B_q[:, tile_n]           # [K_HALF, BLOCK_N]
             b_scale_tile = B_scale[tile_n, :] # [BLOCK_N, K_SCALE] — 2D ✓
             result = hl.dot_scaled(
                 a_tile, a_scale_tile, "e2m1",
                 b_tile, b_scale_tile, "e2m1",
             )
             C[tile_m, tile_n] = result.to(torch.bfloat16)
         return C
     ```
     - Generated Triton code (M=64, K=128, N=128 template):
     ```python
     @triton.jit
     def _helion_mxfp4_gemm(A_q, A_scale, B_q, B_scale, C, _RDIM_SIZE_2: tl.constexpr, _RDIM_SIZE_3: tl.constexpr):
         num_blocks_0 = tl.cdiv(64, _BLOCK_SIZE_0)
         pid_0 = tl.program_id(0) % num_blocks_0
         pid_1 = tl.program_id(0) // num_blocks_0
         offset_0 = pid_0 * _BLOCK_SIZE_0
         indices_0 = (offset_0 + tl.arange(0, _BLOCK_SIZE_0)).to(tl.int32)
         offset_1 = pid_1 * _BLOCK_SIZE_1
         indices_1 = (offset_1 + tl.arange(0, _BLOCK_SIZE_1)).to(tl.int32)
         indices_2 = tl.arange(0, _RDIM_SIZE_2).to(tl.int32)   # K_HALF range
         indices_3 = tl.arange(0, _RDIM_SIZE_3).to(tl.int32)   # K_SCALE range
         a_tile = tl.load(A_q + (indices_0[:, None] * 64 + indices_2[None, :] * 1), None)
         a_scale_tile = tl.load(A_scale + (indices_0[:, None] * 4 + indices_3[None, :] * 1), None)
         b_tile = tl.load(B_q + (indices_2[:, None] * 128 + indices_1[None, :] * 1), None)
         b_scale_tile = tl.load(B_scale + (indices_1[:, None] * 4 + indices_3[None, :] * 1), None)
         result = tl.dot_scaled(a_tile, a_scale_tile, 'e2m1', b_tile, b_scale_tile, 'e2m1')
         v_0 = tl.cast(result, tl.bfloat16)
         v_1 = tl.cast(v_0, tl.float32)
         tl.store(C + (indices_0[:, None] * 128 + indices_1[None, :] * 1), v_1, None)
     ```
     - Key observation: stride for A_q = `indices_0[:, None] * K_HALF + indices_2[None, :] * 1` (K_HALF=64 for K=128); stride for A_scale = `indices_0[:, None] * K_SCALE + indices_3[None, :] * 1` (K_SCALE=4 for K=128)

   - **`helion/_compiler/indexing_strategy.py`** (READ — found root cause)
     - `SubscriptIndexing.compute_shape` determines output shape from index list
     - Key code path for `torch.SymInt` index:
     ```python
     elif isinstance(k, torch.SymInt):
         input_size.popleft()
         symbol = k._sympy_()
         if isinstance(symbol, sympy.Symbol):
             origin = HostFunction.current().expr_to_origin.get(symbol)
             if origin and isinstance(origin.origin, BlockSizeOrigin):
                 output_size.append(k)
         # Note: if not BlockSizeOrigin, this is a scalar index that eliminates the dim
     ```
     - `BLOCK_K // 16` is a `sympy.FloorDiv` expression (not plain `sympy.Symbol`) → `isinstance(symbol, sympy.Symbol)` fails → dimension NOT appended → 1D result

   - **`helion/language/tile_proxy.py`** (READ)
     - `Tile.__torch_function__` handles all ops on Tile objects
     - For `//` (floordiv): falls through to `_tiles_to_sizes(args)` → converts Tile to SymInt → applies `//` → returns derived SymInt
     - `_tiles_to_sizes_for_index` converts `Tile` → `SymInt` for use as indices

   - **`helion/language/memory_ops.py`** (READ)
     - `load` prepare_args: `index = Tile._tiles_to_sizes_for_index(index)` converts tile indices
     - `register_fake`: calls `SubscriptIndexing.compute_shape(tensor, index)` with `state=None` to get fake tensor shape

   - **`helion/language/matmul_ops.py`** (READ)
     - Line 429: `if mat1_scale.ndim != 2: raise ValueError(...)` — the validation check that was failing
     - K enforcement (line 461-466): enforces minimum block size of 32 for K dimension

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission_fused_triton.py`** (PARTIALLY READ)

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`** (READ in prior session)

   The sole explicit user message was: "Continue the conversation from where it left off without asking the user any further questions.    The underlying objective is Phase 16 of the Luma AMD Speedrun competition on the `amd-mxfp4-mm` leaderboard (AMD MI355X, gfx950). Current score: ~13.7µs geomean vs leader 9.7µs. Task #11 (in_progress): Explore further GEMM optimization opportunities via Helion-generated Triton kernels — specifically building a Triton `tl.dot_scaled` GEMM with pre-quantized A (from HIP kernel) and Helion-validated pointer arithmetic.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **`tl.dot_scaled` on gfx950**: LHS scale `[BLOCK_M, SCALE_PER_K]`, RHS scale `[BLOCK_N, SCALE_PER_K]` (N-first hardware requirement). BLOCK_K min = 64 packed fp4x2 bytes. SCALE_PER_K = BLOCK_K // 16.
   - **Transposed B loading in Triton without CPU copy**: Load B_q `[N, K//2]` with transposed strides: `offs_k[:, None] * stride_b_k + offs_n[None, :] * stride_b_n` where stride_b_k=1, stride_b_n=K_HALF. This gives `b_tile[k,n] = B_q[n,k] = B.T[k,n]` — zero CPU-side transpose needed.
   - **HIP linear-scale quant kernel**: Writes A_scale in LINEAR row-major `[M, K//32]` format (vs. shuffled format for aiter ASM path). Direct `row * (K//32) + grp` addressing, no shuffle step, compatible with Triton `tl.dot_scaled`.
   - **B cache stale data bug**: Caching B preprocessing by `(data_ptr, N, K)` causes correctness failures when the test runner generates a fresh B tensor (different random seed) that the allocator happens to place at the same address as a previous tensor. The fix is to not cache B preprocessing.
   - **`dynamic_mxfp4_quant` column-major stride**: Returns A_scale with stride=(1,M) (column-major), requires `.contiguous()` before `.view(torch.uint8)`.
   - **HIP/Triton stream ordering**: `A.contiguous()` as inline expression in ctypes call creates a temporary that could be freed before async kernel completes. Fix: assign to named variable `A_cont = A.contiguous()`.
   - **MXFP4 GEMM pipeline stages**: `num_stages=2-4` benefits MI355X's deep execution pipeline.
   - **`_unshuffle_e8m0`**: Reverses aiter e8m0 shuffle for B_scale: `u8.view(sm//32, sn//8, 4, 16, 2, 2).permute(0,5,3,1,4,2).contiguous().view(sm,sn)[:n,:k//32]`. Cheap (~1-2MB copy), no caching needed.

3. Files and Code Sections:

     - Key autotune pattern and the `_unshuffle_e8m0` function reused.

   - **`kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission_helion_gemm.py`** (discovered existing, then overwritten twice):
     - Original content: Used `dynamic_mxfp4_quant(A)` + no-cache B preprocessing + Triton tl.dot_scaled GEMM. BLOCK_K in [64, 128].
     - **First rewrite (incorrect)**: Added HIP linear-scale kernel, B caching by `(data_ptr,N,K)`, static buffers. Failed test: k=1536, m=64, n=3072 — large errors (26.875 vs 51.75).
     - **Second rewrite (current)**: Fixes both bugs — removes B caching, eliminates B transpose by loading with transposed strides in kernel.

   Current final version of `submission_helion_gemm.py`:
   ```python
   """
   MXFP4 GEMM: HIP linear-scale quant + Triton tl.dot_scaled (no B transpose).
   
   Key optimization: load B_q[N, K//2] with transposed strides inside Triton.
   b_tile[k,n] = B_q[n,k] via offs_k[:,None]*1 + offs_n[None,:]*K_HALF
   Result: zero CPU-side copy needed for B data.
   ...
   """
   # HIP kernel writes A_scale in LINEAR [M, K//32] layout
   # SO file: /tmp/_mxfp4_quant_linear_v2.so
   
   @triton.autotune(configs=_make_configs(), key=["M", "N", "K_HALF"])
   @triton.jit
   def _mxfp4_gemm_kernel(
       A_ptr, A_scale_ptr,
       B_ptr, B_scale_ptr,
       C_ptr,
       M, N, K_HALF,
       stride_a_m, stride_a_k,
       stride_as_m, stride_as_k,
       stride_b_n, stride_b_k,   # B_q [N, K//2]: (K//2, 1) — loaded transposed
       stride_bs_n, stride_bs_k, # B_scale [N, K//32] N-first
       stride_c_m, stride_c_n,
       BLOCK_M: tl.constexpr,
       BLOCK_N: tl.constexpr,
       BLOCK_K: tl.constexpr,    # fp4x2 packed bytes, min=64
       GROUP_SIZE_M: tl.constexpr,
   ):
       SCALE_PER_K: tl.constexpr = BLOCK_K // 16
       # ... group-M swizzle ...
       for k_start in range(0, K_HALF, BLOCK_K):
           offs_k = k_start + tl.arange(0, BLOCK_K)
           offs_ks = (k_start // 16) + tl.arange(0, SCALE_PER_K)
           a = tl.load(A_ptr + offs_m[:,None]*stride_a_m + offs_k[None,:]*stride_a_k, ...)
           a_scale = tl.load(A_scale_ptr + offs_m[:,None]*stride_as_m + offs_ks[None,:]*stride_as_k, ...)
           # B loaded with transposed strides: b_tile[k,n] = B_q[n,k]
           b = tl.load(B_ptr + offs_k[:,None]*stride_b_k + offs_n[None,:]*stride_b_n, ...)
           b_scale = tl.load(B_scale_ptr + offs_n[:,None]*stride_bs_n + offs_ks[None,:]*stride_bs_k, ...)
           acc = tl.dot_scaled(a, a_scale, "e2m1", b, b_scale, "e2m1", acc=acc)
   
   def custom_kernel(data):
       A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
       M, K = A.shape; N = B.shape[0]; K_HALF = K // 2
       B_q_u8 = B_q.view(torch.uint8)  # [N, K//2] — no CPU transpose
       B_scale = _unshuffle_e8m0(B_scale_sh, N, K)  # [N, K//32]
       lib = _ensure_hip()
       if lib is not None:
           A_q, A_scale = _get_a_bufs(M, K)
           out = _get_out_buf(M, N)
           A_cont = A.contiguous()  # named var — keeps reference alive for async HIP
           err = lib.launch_mxfp4_quant_linear(A_cont.data_ptr(), A_q.data_ptr(), A_scale.data_ptr(), M, K)
           if err == 0:
               _mxfp4_gemm_kernel[grid](A_q, A_scale, B_q_u8, B_scale, out, M, N, K_HALF,
                   A_q.stride(0), A_q.stride(1),
                   A_scale.stride(0), A_scale.stride(1),
                   B_q_u8.stride(0), B_q_u8.stride(1),  # (K_HALF, 1) — transposed in kernel
                   B_scale.stride(0), B_scale.stride(1),
                   out.stride(0), out.stride(1))
               return out
       # Fallback: dynamic_mxfp4_quant + Triton GEMM
   ```

   The `_make_configs()` generates: BLOCK_M=[16,32,64,128], BLOCK_N=[64,128,256], BLOCK_K=[64,128,256] (fp4x2 packed bytes), num_warps=[4,8], num_stages=[2,3,4], skipping BLOCK_M=128+BLOCK_N=256 and BLOCK_K=256+BLOCK_N=256.

   The HIP kernel (`_mxfp4_quant_linear_v2.so`) writes:
   ```c
   A_scale[row * num_groups_per_row + grp] = scale_byte;  // LINEAR, row-major [M, K//32]
   ```
   vs. submission.py which writes with `shuffle_index(row, grp, sm, sn)` for aiter ASM compatibility.

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **`submission_helion_gemm.py` correctness failure (k=1536, m=64, n=3072)**:
     - Symptom: Large errors (26.875 vs 51.75) on m=64 shape, while m=8, m=16, m=256 pass.
     - Root cause: B cache keyed on `(B_q.data_ptr(), N, K)`. Test runner generates fresh B for each test case with different random seed. When (N=3072, K=1536) repeats for m=16 then m=64, the allocator may reuse the same data_ptr. The m=16 result is cached; m=64 gets stale cached B_t/B_scale from m=16's B — wrong weights, massive errors.
     - Fix: Remove B preprocessing cache entirely. B_scale unshuffle is ~0.1µs (negligible). B_q is now loaded with transposed strides in Triton (zero CPU copy), so no B transpose needed at all.
   - **`A.contiguous()` inline in ctypes call**: Creates a temporary tensor whose reference drops to zero immediately, potentially freed before async HIP kernel completes.
     - Fix: `A_cont = A.contiguous()` keeps reference alive through the function scope.

5. Problem Solving:
   - **Helion `mat1_scale must be 2D` error** (from previous session): Fixed by using `A_scale[tile_m, :]` (full slice) instead of `A_scale[tile_m, tile_k // 16]`. The derived FloorDiv SymInt from `tile_k // 16` fails the `BlockSizeOrigin` check in `SubscriptIndexing.compute_shape`, producing a 1D tensor. The full-slice approach creates a reduction dimension, producing [BLOCK_M, K_SCALE] 2D tensor.
   - **B transpose elimination**: Key insight — Triton can load B_q `[N, K//2]` as a `[BLOCK_K, BLOCK_N]` tile with transposed strides without any CPU-side memory copy. Eliminates 1-5µs B transpose overhead for large shapes.
   - **B cache correctness**: Removed B caching to prevent stale data when test runner regenerates B with different seeds but possibly same memory address.
   - **HIP linear A_scale**: New HIP kernel writes A_scale in row-major `[M, K//32]` format, directly usable by Triton without unshuffle step (unlike aiter shuffled format).

Summary:

   The session resumed Phase 16 of the Luma AMD Speedrun competition (Luma AMD Speedrun, leaderboard: amd-mxfp4-mm). 2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **MXFP4 GEMM pipeline**: HIP fused quant+shuffle kernel → `aiter.gemm_a4w4_asm` (CK assembly)
   - **`tl.dot_scaled` on gfx950**: Triton approach confirmed slower than aiter CK assembly
   - **`log2_k_split` parameter**: Controls 2^n split-K groups in `gemm_a4w4_asm`
   - **aiter KSPLIT semantics**: `AITER_KSPLIT` env var switches between CK MXFP4-optimized path (0) vs cktile path (>0) in `fused_moe`
   - **Expert-count-aware KSPLIT**: 257-expert shapes benefit from KSPLIT=4; 32-expert shapes MUST use KSPLIT=2 (KSPLIT=4 causes catastrophic overflow on 32-expert/dexp=512)
   - **`AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG`**: Forces heuristic path instead of CSV-tuned configs
   - **`AITER_USE_NT=1`**: Enables non-temporal memory loads
   - **Catastrophic overflow bug**: KSPLIT=4 for 32-expert shapes produces ~1e27 values (vs expected ~0.015 max error) due to incorrect accumulation in cktile reduction
   - **aiter CK ASM kernels**: `f4gemm_bf16_per1x32Fp4_BpreShuffle_32x128` and `192x128` tiles loaded at runtime via `hipModuleLoad`
   - **Geometric mean**: Used for all competition scoring (not arithmetic mean)

8. Current Work:
   Immediately before the summary request, was attempting to write the fixed MoE production `submission.py` with the correct expert-count-aware KSPLIT logic. The Write tool returned an error: `File has not been read yet. Read it first before writing to it.`

   The intended fix file content (complete, ready to write after reading):
   ```python
   """
   MXFP4 MoE: Expert-count-aware adaptive KSPLIT + NT.

   Strategy:
   - Dense (est_m >= 50): CK MXFP4-optimized path (no bypass, no ksplit)
   - 257-expert, very sparse (est_m < 10): KSPLIT=4 for K-parallelism
   - All other sparse shapes (including 32-expert): KSPLIT=2

   This prevents the catastrophic overflow (~1e27) that occurs when
   KSPLIT=4 is used with 32-expert shapes (dexp=512) where K/4=128 is
   too small for the cktile reduction to handle correctly.
   """
   import os
   from task import input_t, output_t
   from aiter import ActivationType, QuantType
   from aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe

   os.environ["AITER_USE_NT"] = "1"

   _state: dict = {"ksplit": None}

   def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
       (
           hidden_states, gate_up_weight, down_weight,
           gate_up_weight_scale, down_weight_scale,
           gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
           gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled, down_weight_scale_shuffled,
           topk_weights, topk_ids, config,
       ) = data

       hidden_pad = config["d_hidden_pad"] - config["d_hidden"]
       intermediate_pad = config["d_expert_pad"] - config["d_expert"]

       num_experts = gate_up_weight_shuffled.shape[0]
       estimated_m = topk_ids.numel() // num_experts

       # Expert-count-aware KSPLIT selection (from empirical benchmarking)
       if estimated_m >= 50:
           # Dense shapes: CK MXFP4-optimized path is fastest
           ks = "default"
       elif num_experts >= 200 and estimated_m < 10:
           # 257-expert very sparse: KSPLIT=4 for K=7168 parallelism
           ks = "4"
       else:
           # All other sparse shapes (32-expert, moderate density): KSPLIT=2
           ks = "2"

       if _state["ksplit"] != ks:
           if ks == "default":
               os.environ.pop("AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG", None)
               os.environ.pop("AITER_KSPLIT", None)
           else:
               os.environ["AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG"] = "1"
               os.environ["AITER_KSPLIT"] = ks
           _state["ksplit"] = ks

       return fused_moe(
           hidden_states, gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
           topk_weights, topk_ids, expert_mask=None,
           activation=ActivationType.Silu, quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32,
           doweight_stage1=False,
           w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
           w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled,
           a1_scale=None, a2_scale=None,
           hidden_pad=hidden_pad, intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad,
       )
   ```

# /learn - Online Learning System

**Extract reusable knowledge from this session into skills.** Evaluates what was learned, checks for existing skills, and creates new ones when valuable patterns are discovered.

---

## TABLE OF CONTENTS

| Phase       | Description                                            | Steps   |
| ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------ | ------- |
| **Phase 0** | Reference: triggers, quality criteria, skill structure | 0.1–0.4 |
| **Phase 1** | Evaluate: assess if knowledge is worth extracting      | 1.1     |
| **Phase 2** | Check Existing: search for related skills              | 2.1     |
| **Phase 3** | Create Skill: write the skill file                     | 3.1–3.2 |
| **Phase 4** | Quality Gates: final checklist before saving           | 4.1     |

**Quick Step Reference:**

- 0.1 Triggers, 0.2 Quality criteria, 0.3 What NOT to extract, 0.4 Skill structure
- 1.1 Self-evaluation questions
- 2.1 Search existing skills
- 3.1 Choose location, 3.2 Write SKILL.md
- 4.1 Final checklist

---

## PHASE 0: REFERENCE

### Step 0.1: Triggers

Invoke `/learn` after ANY task involving:

| Trigger                      | Example                                                          |
| ---------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Non-obvious debugging**    | Spent 10+ minutes investigating; solution wasn't in docs         |
| **Misleading errors**        | Error message pointed wrong direction; found real cause          |
| **Workarounds**              | Found limitation and creative solution                           |
| **Tool integration**         | Figured out how to use tool/API in undocumented way              |
| **Trial-and-error**          | Tried multiple approaches before finding what worked             |
| **Repeatable workflow**      | Multi-step task that will recur; worth standardizing             |
| **External service queries** | Fetched data from Jira, GitHub, Confluence, or other APIs        |
| **User-facing automation**   | Built something user will ask for again (reports, status checks) |

### Step 0.2: Quality Criteria

Skills must be:

- **Reusable**: Will help with future tasks (not just this instance)
- **Non-trivial**: Required discovery, OR is a valuable workflow pattern
- **Verified**: Solution actually worked, not theoretical

### Step 0.3: What NOT to Extract

- Single-step tasks with no workflow value
- One-off fixes unlikely to recur
- Knowledge easily found in official docs

### Step 0.4: Skill Structure

**Location:** `.claude/skills/[skill-name]/SKILL.md`

**Template:**

```markdown
---
name: descriptive-kebab-case-name
description: |
  [CRITICAL: This determines when the skill triggers. Include:]
  - What the skill does
  - Specific trigger conditions (exact error messages, symptoms)
  - When to use it (contexts, scenarios)
author: Claude Code
version: 1.0.0
---

# Skill Name

## Problem

[Clear description of the problem]

## Context / Trigger Conditions

[When to use - exact error messages, symptoms, scenarios]

## Solution

[Step-by-step solution]

## Verification

[How to verify it worked]

## Example

[Concrete example]

## References

[Links to documentation]
```

<details>
<summary>Writing Effective Descriptions</summary>

The description field is CRITICAL for skill discovery:

**Good:**

```yaml
description: |
  Fix for "ENOENT: no such file or directory" errors in npm monorepos.
  Use when: (1) npm run fails with ENOENT, (2) paths work in root but
  not in packages, (3) symlinked dependencies cause failures.
```

**Bad:**

```yaml
description: Helps with npm problems in monorepos.
```

</details>

**Guidelines:**

- **Concise** - Claude is smart; only add what it doesn't know
- **Under 500 lines** - Move large docs to `references/`
- **Examples over explanations** - Show, don't tell

---

## PHASE 1: EVALUATE

### Step 1.1: Self-Evaluation Questions

Ask yourself:

1. "What did I just learn that wasn't obvious before starting?"
2. "Would future-me benefit from having this documented?"
3. "Was the solution non-obvious from documentation alone?"
4. "Is this a multi-step workflow I'd repeat on similar tasks?"
5. "Did I query an external service the user will ask about again?"

**If NO to all → Skip extraction, nothing to learn.**

**Note:** External service queries (Jira tickets, GitHub PRs, Confluence pages) are almost always worth extracting - users frequently repeat these requests.

---

## PHASE 2: CHECK EXISTING

### Step 2.1: Search for Related Skills

Before creating, search for related skills:

```bash
ls .claude/skills/ 2>/dev/null
rg -i "keyword" .claude/skills/ 2>/dev/null
ls ~/.claude/pilot/skills/ 2>/dev/null
rg -i "keyword" ~/.claude/pilot/skills/ 2>/dev/null
```

| Found                | Action                           |
| -------------------- | -------------------------------- |
| Nothing related      | Create new skill                 |
| Same trigger and fix | Update existing (bump version)   |
| Partial overlap      | Update existing with new variant |

---

## PHASE 3: CREATE SKILL

### Step 3.1: Choose Location

**Project skills**: `.claude/skills/[skill-name]/SKILL.md`

### Step 3.2: Write SKILL.md

Use the template from Step 0.4. Ensure the description field contains specific trigger conditions.

---

## PHASE 4: QUALITY GATES

### Step 4.1: Final Checklist

Before finalizing:

- [ ] Description contains specific trigger conditions
- [ ] Solution verified to work
- [ ] Content specific enough to be actionable
- [ ] Content general enough to be reusable
- [ ] No sensitive information

---

## EXAMPLE

**Scenario**: Discovered LSP `findReferences` can find dead code by checking if functions have only 1 reference (their definition) or only test references.

**Skill Created**: `.claude/skills/lsp-dead-code-finder/SKILL.md`

```markdown
---
name: lsp-dead-code-finder
description: |
  Find dead/unused code using LSP findReferences. Use when: (1) user asks
  to find dead code, (2) cleaning up codebase, (3) refactoring. Key insight:
  function with only 1 reference (definition) or only test refs is dead code.
---

# LSP Dead Code Finder

...
```

---

## REMEMBER

**Continuous improvement.** Every valuable discovery should benefit future sessions.
Evaluate after significant work. Extract selectively. Create carefully.

---

Are we doing custom triton kernels with helion?

---

What's our plan for achieving top ten on all 3 leaderboards?

---

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The session resumed Phase 16 of the Luma AMD Speedrun competition. Initial task was fixing a critical regression in MoE `submission.py` (broken KSPLIT threshold causing catastrophic overflow). After fixing that, the user requested hash-based HIP filenames to prevent cross-session collisions, then invoked `/learn` to extract skills. The user then asked about Helion/Triton kernel strategy (answered: confirmed slower than CK ASM). Finally, the user entered **plan mode** asking: **"What's our plan for achieving top ten on all 3 leaderboards?"** — this is the current active request requiring a comprehensive new plan.

   Current leaderboard standings:
   - **GEMM**: ~12.9µs geomean (leader: 9.7µs, 1.33x gap)
   - **MLA**: ~69.5µs ranked geomean (leader: 4.3µs, 16x gap)
   - **MoE**: ~157µs geomean (leader: ~145µs, 1.08x gap)

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **MXFP4 GEMM pipeline**: HIP fused quant+shuffle → `aiter.gemm_a4w4_asm` (CK assembly)
   - **Expert-count-aware KSPLIT**: est_m≥50→default(CK), 257E+est_m<10→KSPLIT=4, else→KSPLIT=2
   - **KSPLIT=4 catastrophic overflow**: For 32-expert/dexp=512, K/4=128 too small for cktile reduction → ~1e27 output values
   - **Hash-based HIP `.so` naming**: `md5(HIP_SRC)[:8]` prevents stale cache and cross-session collisions
   - **`doweight_stage1=True` with SiLU**: Mathematically incorrect (nonlinear activation order matters), must be False
   - **Triton vs CK ASM**: Triton `tl.dot_scaled` confirmed 1.34x slower than CK assembly for GEMM; custom Triton MLA 9-127x slower than torch.einsum (decode is GEMV not GEMM)
   - **MLA decode**: GEMV problem (single query row), matrix cores need 16×16 minimum tiles, wastes 15/16 compute
   - **Popcorn CLI submission**: 12-minute workflow timeout, JIT builds consume ~160s for MoE (sorting+cktile+activation)

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission.py`** (FIXED — critical regression repair):
     - Was broken: `if estimated_m >= 100: ks = "default"; else: ks = "4"` — KSPLIT=4 for ALL sparse shapes caused 1e27 overflow on 32-expert/dexp=512
     - Fixed to expert-count-aware logic. Also reverted external `doweight_stage1=True` back to `False`
     - Final working version:
     ```python
     """
     MXFP4 MoE: Expert-count-aware adaptive KSPLIT + NT.
     
     Strategy:
     - Dense (est_m >= 50): CK MXFP4-optimized path (no bypass, no ksplit)
     - 257-expert, very sparse (est_m < 10): KSPLIT=4 for K-parallelism
     - All other sparse shapes (including 32-expert): KSPLIT=2
     
     KSPLIT=4 causes catastrophic overflow (~1e27) for 32-expert shapes (dexp=512)
     where K/4=128 is too small for the cktile reduction to handle correctly.
     Only safe for 257-expert shapes where dexp=256 (K/4=64 fits cktile).
     """
     import os
     from task import input_t, output_t
     from aiter import ActivationType, QuantType
     from aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe
     
     os.environ["AITER_USE_NT"] = "1"
     
     _state: dict = {"ksplit": None}
     
     def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
         (
             hidden_states, gate_up_weight, down_weight,
             gate_up_weight_scale, down_weight_scale,
             gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
             gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled, down_weight_scale_shuffled,
             topk_weights, topk_ids, config,
         ) = data
     
         hidden_pad = config["d_hidden_pad"] - config["d_hidden"]
         intermediate_pad = config["d_expert_pad"] - config["d_expert"]
     
         num_experts = gate_up_weight_shuffled.shape[0]
         estimated_m = topk_ids.numel() // num_experts
     
         if estimated_m >= 50:
             ks = "default"
         elif num_experts >= 200 and estimated_m < 10:
             ks = "4"
         else:
             ks = "2"
     
         if _state["ksplit"] != ks:
             if ks == "default":
                 os.environ.pop("AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG", None)
                 os.environ.pop("AITER_KSPLIT", None)
             else:
                 os.environ["AITER_BYPASS_TUNE_CONFIG"] = "1"
                 os.environ["AITER_KSPLIT"] = ks
             _state["ksplit"] = ks
     
         return fused_moe(
             hidden_states, gate_up_weight_shuffled, down_weight_shuffled,
             topk_weights, topk_ids, expert_mask=None,
             activation=ActivationType.Silu, quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32,
             doweight_stage1=False,
             w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
             w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled,
             a1_scale=None, a2_scale=None,
             hidden_pad=hidden_pad, intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad,
         )
     ```
     - MoE test: ✅ 3/3, leaderboard: ✅ (bs=16/257E=94.7µs, bs=128/257E=181µs, bs=512/257E=274µs, bs=16/33E=64.3µs, bs=128/33E=111µs, bs=512/33E-dexp512=210µs)

     ```python
     def _ensure_hip():
         global _hip_lib, _hip_done
         if _hip_done:
             return _hip_lib
         _hip_done = True
         import hashlib
         src_hash = hashlib.md5(HIP_SRC.encode()).hexdigest()[:8]
         src = f"/tmp/_mxfp4_quant_{src_hash}.hip"
         so = f"/tmp/_mxfp4_quant_{src_hash}.so"
     ```
     - Test: ✅ 4/4 (max error 0.0)
